# ONECALL project execution control

> Controller: Phaseflow
>
> Design source: `docs/12-technology-stack-and-system-architecture.md`
>
> Branch: `codex/a2-production-ivr-protocol-spec`
>
> Dispatch protocol: `sub-agents` via one-shot runner processes
>
> Dispatch providers: default `ds` using API model ID `deepseek-flash` (DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash); `mimo26` uses `mimo-v2.6-pro`; the user-selected coding pair adds `mimo26flash` using `mimo-v2.6-flash`. For paired implementation review, `ds` authors are reviewed by a fresh `mimo26flash` task and `mimo26flash` authors by a fresh `ds` task; fixes return to the author provider and re-reviews to the reviewer provider. All use `claude-agent-run` while the Codex Responses adapter cannot complete write-tool result correlation.

## Project objective

Turn the reviewed ONECALL product rules and target architecture into production-ready, testable increments without treating the existing HTML prototypes as production code. Each work unit follows the Gateflow order independently and must preserve the module ownership, trust boundaries, state machines and deferred production decisions in the design source.

## Execution order

The architecture design defines the authoritative coarse order:

1. Phase A: platform foundation.
2. Phase B: IVR task, trial and evidence closure.
3. Phase C: customer purchase and recording sharing.
4. Phase D: AI, notary and production readiness.

Before production implementation, section 18 of the design source requires code-ready specifications in this order: data model and initial schema, OpenAPI surfaces, IVR protocol, security design, capacity/deployment sizing, and the production acceptance matrix. These are delivered as bounded work units rather than one oversized implementation phase.

- Motivation and direct evidence: A1 deliberately leaves the detailed IVR session, event, retry, recovery and media protocol to A2/Q15/Q26/Q28. The accepted API surface exposes resolve, activation confirmation, outbound authorization, call event, recording declaration and recording completion operations, while the existing IVR design already fixes one nonterminal task per normalized caller, a configured-and-frozen 30-second default task TTL, atomic claim/reservation/frozen action, task-path automatic B-leg dialing, direct-path DTMF flow and IVR ownership of telephony/media facts. A2 must close the deterministic wire and recovery contract before runtime implementation.

### A2 binding goal contract

- Goal: produce a code-generation-ready, versioned IVR protocol specification that tells the existing self-built IVR and ONECALL backend exactly how to coordinate from inbound session creation through unified action execution, activation re-decision, B-leg recovery, bridge/recording events, heartbeat, terminal settlement and recording media upload.
- Success signal: every protocol message has a stable identity, owner and state precondition; initial `resolve` and post-activation re-decision share one action-selection contract; normal, duplicate, delayed, reordered, conflicting and lost-response paths are explicit; an IVR B-leg operation is exactly-once from the IVR perspective; terminal, recording and media states map exactly to A0 and A1; unknown outcomes fail closed and remain recoverable without redial or double charging; protocol compatibility, stable errors and deterministic static validation or executable fixtures are defined.
- Scope boundary: protocol specification and deterministic static validation/fixtures only. The work unit may add the canonical IVR protocol specification, bounded machine-readable fixtures or validators, Gateflow artifacts and controller updates. A1 schemas may be refined only if an unavoidable contradiction is proven and separately reviewed; additive protocol detail should otherwise map to the accepted A1 surface.
- Non-goals: no Java/runtime service implementation, IVR media-engine rewrite, SIP/provider-specific telephony design, Tencent Cloud resources, actual credentials, production numeric security values, UI/prototype changes, payment/order flows, PBX/extensions/seat PIN, operator-system integration, capacity/deployment sizing or production acceptance matrix.
- No over-design: specify only the lifecycle, sequencing, identity, idempotency, recovery, compatibility and error rules required by the approved IVR behavior. Keep security key hierarchy, numeric replay windows, duration tolerances, authorization windows and provider details as typed deferred inputs instead of inventing Q7/Q8/Q10/Q18/Q25/Q27 values.
- Blocking open questions: none for planning. Q15/Q26/Q28 protocol shapes and recovery semantics are the work unit's target; unresolved numeric, provider and policy values remain named deferred inputs and cannot be guessed by the plan.

### A2 project constraints

- The self-built IVR already owns inbound answering, outbound B-leg dialing, bridging, recording, timed hangup, call facts and media upload. The ONECALL backend owns activation, outbound tasks, entitlement, reservation/settlement and evidence state; operator systems do not participate.
- One normalized caller may have only one nonterminal outbound task. Task creation replaces the prior pending task; default expiry is 30 seconds, server-configured and frozen on that task. Resolve atomically claims the task, reserves entitlement and returns the frozen action. The IVR executes that action and must not independently recompute routing or select a different callee.
- The task path automatically dials the frozen callee after one successful inbound claim; it has no callee confirmation in the voice call. The no-task direct path may collect and confirm a callee through DTMF under its separate authorization flow.
- The same `callId` roots the inbound session, task claim, B-leg operation, legs, bridge, quota reservation, terminal facts, recording, media object and evidence chain. Stable event/operation identities must distinguish exact replay from semantic conflict.
- Initial resolve and activation confirmation must converge on one shared action-selection result shape and invariant set. Activation confirmation cannot silently create a separate routing policy or bypass the original call/session boundary.
- Lost backend responses, delayed/reordered callbacks and IVR restart/recovery must not cause a second B-leg dial, a second reservation/settlement or contradictory terminal/media facts. Unknown telephony outcomes fail closed until authoritative reconciliation.
- A0 persistence ownership and state machines and A1 paths/schemas/errors remain authoritative. The protocol must not expose COS object keys, permanent media URLs, credentials, raw security material or provider internals.
- Existing HTML prototypes, `shared/onecall-business.js`, modified QA screenshots and untracked `docs/reviews/plan-review-20261021-205118.md` are outside A2 and must remain untouched and unstaged.

### A2 slice principle

Implementation slices must be behaviorally verifiable and minimize Gateflow review cost. Group protocol work by coherent end-to-end behavior and failure recovery, not mechanically by files, message types or IVR/backend ownership. Prefer no more than three implementation slices; a plan proposing more must prove why they cannot be safely combined without weakening validation or mixing incompatible state transitions.

The fix must define exact stage ownership: S1 may register event identities/names and validate the S1-approved projection, while S2 must add/freeze payload/state semantics and promote the event block to its exact full form; the validator must reject premature future-slice fields and missing current-stage fields. The fix must state exact per-stage model/doc/fixture expectations and counts without moving S2 protocol design into S1.
- Review open question 2 `accepted` as a bounded negative fixture clarification: when resolve never committed, there is no backend call/reservation, so a later `OUTBOUND_NOT_STARTED` event returns `404 CALL_UNKNOWN`, becomes a permanent outbox failure and cannot be represented as release. This does not change the existing fail-closed protocol; add the explicit scenario/assertion and adjust exact stage fixture IDs/counts consistently.
- Open question 1 (confirm/direct unknown call maps to A1-forced 409) is `deferred-with-owner`: IVR/Backend observability and possible later A1 refinement; A2 must not invent a new error. The reviewer tooling-access note is controller-observed rather than a product finding: the controller read the complete Planreview skill and embedded its mandatory lenses in the task, so no plan scope change is required.
- All other review residuals remain classified with the plan's owners/destinations: task ID presentation, heartbeat volume/retention, declaration replay, session abandonment, upload observation, evidence handoff, static validator co-authorship, caller correlation, line/CDR runtime recovery and deferred Q-values. None authorizes an A0/A1/prototype/runtime change in this fix.

#### A2 S1 code-review result and bounded-fix adjudication

- The first MiMo task `a2-ivr-s1-review-mimo26flash-20261025-01` exited 0 at the wrapper layer but returned `stop_reason=content_filter` with only a rejection message and no artifact, so it did not satisfy the review completion contract. The single same-provider retry `a2-ivr-s1-review-mimo26flash-20261025-01-retry1` completed normally: run `/tmp/sub-agents.a2-ivr-s1-review-retry1.rhE8Zh`, outer/recorded exit 0, valid JSON `subtype=success`, `is_error=false`, `stop_reason=end_turn`, non-empty result and empty stderr.
- Review artifact `docs/reviews/code-review-20261025-000422.md` has SHA-256 `F1281C417256F021286F51054AD27BD302CF249E3FABBC076520CE8B581E4F5A` (13239 bytes / 145 lines), covers all ten S1 paths and concludes `pass-with-risks` with two low findings and no blocking question. The reviewer could not execute Node/npm, but the controller reran the S1 validator twice after review; both runs exited 0 with the exact accepted counters and byte-identical output.
- `accepted` F1 in substance: `validateFixture(entry.fixture, 's1', ...)` makes the shared cumulative runner reject every future `slice=s2`/`slice=s3` fixture. The review's literal suggestion to pass the current stage is not accepted because stage S2/S3 also revalidates earlier S1 fixtures. The bounded fix must derive `expectedSlice` from the fixture's exact registered ID prefix constrained by `config.sliceOrder`, so S2 accepts S1+S2 and S3 accepts S1+S2+S3 while unknown/mismatched IDs still fail closed.
- `rejected-with-reason` F2: adding one of the three forbidden domain reasons to A1 `ProblemCode` while keeping the model valid triggers the existing conditional. Removing it from the model cannot evade validation because `IvrDecision.reasonCode` is deep-equaled with `model.decisionReasons` and the model registry is set-equaled with A0 `ck_ivr_decision_reason`. The review demonstrates a secondary assertion that cannot fail alone, not an overall validator false negative; changing it adds no required correctness.
- Fix scope is exactly `protocol/ivr/validate.mjs`, `docs/gateflow/a2-production-ivr-protocol-spec-s1-implementation.md` and a new `docs/gateflow/a2-production-ivr-protocol-spec-s1-fix-1.md`. The fix must not add S2/S3 model sections, fixtures or behavior, modify A0/A1/prototype files, stage/commit/push, or enter re-review. Required evidence is the baseline S1 validator twice, `npm run check:s3`, and focused temporary-copy probes for S1/S2/S3 mixed-slice acceptance plus unknown/mismatched slice rejection.

- Motivation and direct evidence: the architecture requires OpenAPI contracts for customer, operations, IVR, public sharing and callbacks immediately after the data-model specification. The repository has no production Maven/Spring project, existing OpenAPI/Swagger source or generated client; its HTML prototypes and QA fixtures are behavior evidence only.

### A1 binding goal contract

- Goal: produce a code-generation-ready, machine-readable OpenAPI 3.1 contract source and the minimum companion mapping/validation artifacts for the five approved trust surfaces: customer (`/api/v1/customer/**`), operations (`/api/v1/operations/**`), self-built IVR (`/api/v1/ivr/**`), public recording shares (`/api/v1/public/shares/**`) and authenticated provider callbacks (`/api/v1/callbacks/**`).
- Success signal: every approved first-release operation has an explicit owner surface, authentication/trust requirement, server-derived authorization boundary, request/response schema, stable status and RFC 9457 error mapping, idempotency or event identity semantics for writes, cursor semantics where a list is exposed, and direct mapping to the accepted A0 data/state contracts. Generated TypeScript-client compatibility and OpenAPI validation are specified and reproducible without claiming a runtime service exists.
- Scope boundary: document/API-contract design and static validation only. The work unit may add canonical OpenAPI source, bounded explanatory or validation artifacts, Gateflow artifacts and controller updates. The interface contracts must not contradict the A0 schema or approved customer/IVR/share flows.
- Non-goals: no backend/Maven project, executable controller/service code, Flyway migration, UI/prototype change, payment or identity-provider integration, cloud resource, vendor credential, provider-specific callback payload, detailed IVR session/retry/media protocol, security numeric policy (token TTL, replay window, KDF/rate limits), organization-role matrix, online work-order/refund/invoice workflow or microservice split.
- No over-design: use REST/JSON, OpenAPI 3.1 and RFC 9457 as required by the architecture; model only approved first-release verbs and state transitions. Keep unresolved values and exact provider/policy formats as explicit typed inputs or later-work-unit destinations instead of inventing security values, vendors, configurations or generic workflow endpoints.
- Blocking open questions: none for the surface contract. Q1/Q6/Q7/Q8/Q10/Q12/Q14/Q15/Q18/Q19/Q22/Q25/Q26/Q27/Q28 remain named, owned inputs. A1 may define safe interface boundaries and stable business errors around them, but must not select their unapproved values or detailed protocols.

### A1 project constraints

- Customer, employee, IVR service, public capability and callback identities stay separate; client input never establishes account, workspace, role, price, payment success, evidence solidification or recording ownership.
- IVR inbound routing must preserve the one-normalized-caller/one-nonterminal-task invariant, 30-second configured-and-frozen task expiry baseline, atomic claim/reservation/frozen authorization, no-task direct-trial flow and no implicit latest-callee fallback.
- Sharing must preserve verification modes `NONE`, code, designated mobile and enterprise member; page open/prefetch cannot consume admission, while atomic admission/replay/revocation generation remain server enforced.
- Operations keeps only fixed `ADMIN` and `OPS` roles; employee authentication source and MFA are deferred. Operations metadata access does not imply recording playback.
- Payment remains customer mini-program/Web initiated and server-confirmed; IVR only gives purchase guidance. Work orders, refunds and invoices remain offline support entries, not online workflows.
- A0 supplies the accepted persistence names, immutable facts, state machines and concurrency/ownership constraints. An API projection cannot expose COS keys, raw credentials, protected personal data, internal SQL structures or internal/provider failure detail.
- Existing HTML prototypes, `shared/onecall-business.js`, modified QA screenshots and untracked `docs/reviews/plan-review-20261021-205118.md` are outside A1 and must remain untouched and unstaged.

### A1 slice principle

Implementation slices must be behaviorally verifiable and minimize Gateflow review cost. The planning Agent must group the canonical contract and explanatory validation by cohesive external behavior rather than mechanically by files or API namespaces; a plan proposing more than three slices must explain why they cannot be safely combined.

#### A1 re-review round 3 dispatch health and retry

- Initial task `a1-openapi-planreview-ds-20261023-03` used Claude Code / `ds` / DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash and remained alive for 4:03:14 without a completion event, repository artifact, stdout result or stderr. The Claude child was sleeping in `epoll_wait`, had no child tool process or open network socket at the diagnostic snapshot, and both `/tmp/a1-openapi-planreview3.UPdzUI/result.json` and `stderr.log` remained zero bytes.
- The user explicitly instructed `中断并重试`. The controller sent the interrupt; the wrapper returned exit `1`, and PIDs 161359/161361/161373 no longer exist. No review artifact or A1 plan change is attributable to the interrupted run.
- Per the Sub-agents retry contract, one same-provider retry is authorized with a new instance/run directory, targeted read discipline and a one-hour watchdog so another provider/CLI hang terminates with explicit timeout evidence instead of remaining indefinitely in-flight. This is the only same-provider retry for this dispatch; a second failure must be adjudicated before any provider switch.
- DeepSeek retry task `a1-openapi-planreview-ds-20261023-03-retry1` also remained alive without stdout, stderr or repository artifact until the user instructed the controller to use the newly configured MiMo Agent for review. The controller interrupted it as the explicit provider switch; `/tmp/a1-openapi-planreview3-retry.LwfCdX/result.json` and `stderr.log` remained zero bytes and no process survived.
- The new `mimo26` provider maps to `https://token-plan-cn.xiaomimimo.com/anthropic`, model `mimo-v2.6-pro` and environment credential `MIMO_PLAN_API_KEY`. A no-tool health check exited 0 with valid `success`, `MIMO26_AGENT_OK`, `stop_reason=end_turn` and empty stderr.
- The first formal MiMo review invocation did not reach the model: `claude-agent-run` placed the positional prompt after variadic `--add-dir`, so Claude consumed the prompt as another directory and waited for stdin. A bounded probe reproduced the launcher error. The controller patched the user-local runner to insert the option terminator `--` before the prompt; this changed no repository file. A two-turn `Read`-tool probe then exited 0 with `MIMO26_TOOL_OK`, model `mimo-v2.6-pro` and empty stderr. The failed review invocation had zero-byte outputs and wrote no artifact.
- The single justified MiMo retry `a1-openapi-planreview-mimo26-20261023-01-retry1` completed normally in 55 turns. Its outer process exited 0; `result.json` has `subtype=success`, `is_error=false`, `stop_reason=end_turn`, the actual model name `mimo-v2.6-pro` and a non-empty completion report; stderr is empty. #### A1 S3 code-review result and finding adjudication

- Fresh reviewer task `a1-openapi-s3-review-ds-20261024-01` used Claude Code / `ds` / actual model `deepseek-flash[1m]`, exited successfully and created only `docs/reviews/code-review-20261024-181047.md`. Its independent conclusion is fail with eleven findings; its inability to execute the dynamic suite is not treated as controller validation evidence.
- Controller accepts F-01 through F-11 after direct reconciliation against the accepted A1 plan, A0 tables/transactions and docs/13. The fixes remain within the approved five-surface static contract; no runtime implementation, new operation, provider choice, security numeric policy or UI work is authorized.
- F-01 resolution is explicit: public/IVR `taskId`, `callId`, `recordingId` and `shareId` expose the A0 protocol identities (`outbound_call_task.task_id`, `call_record.call_id`/`inbound_session.call_id`, `recording.recording_id`, `evidence_share_grant.share_id`) with accepted bounds 1-64/1-128/1-128/1-128. They do not expose internal UUID primary keys. The contradictory A0 §10.14 `task.taskId = uuid` projection line is classified as an A0 documentation residual owned by A0/IVR contract maintenance; it does not override the accepted A1 §4.10 identity rule or A0 table definitions.
- F-03 correction must make first-result and replay payloads distinguishable without reconstructing secrets. C15 201 uses a satisfiable first-creation schema that requires `capabilityToken` and conditionally carries `extractionCode` only for `EXTRACTION_CODE`; C15 200 uses metadata-only `ShareState`. C18 keeps one operation/status contract but uses a closed discriminated result (`ROTATED` requires the one-time `extractionCode`; `REPLAYED` forbids it), never returns `capabilityToken`, and updates generated compatibility assertions and docs.
- F-06 resolves the source ambiguity in favor of the binding plan: `protocolVersion` is required IVR input, length 1-32, authenticated/fingerprinted rather than server-derived. Event `sequenceNo` is optional with minimum 1, payload is required, `legId` is 1-64 and task `expiresAt` is non-null.
- F-09 is accepted in full because docs/13 §6.4 and A0 §9.14/§10.17 provide sufficient evidence: I2 `decision: ACCEPT` becomes required; I3 carries required `promptVersion`, `confirmationKey` and `confirmationContent` in addition to event/callee so the frozen fingerprint is representable. Exact future allowed-key negotiation remains Q15/Q26 and must not be invented.
- F-11 is accepted because P2/P3 re-check current token/grant/generation state after page open; race outcomes cannot be delegated solely to P1. Their machine-readable inventories, declared responses, exact-set validator and docs must agree. P3 continues to omit `SHARE_LOCKED` because an already admitted valid session remains eligible.

### A1 plan-review finding adjudication

| Finding | Decision | Required correction / destination |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F-01 | accepted | Remove C19/O18 support-entry operations. The approved unified offline contact/instructions remain UI/deployment configuration, not an anonymous persisted-business API or a fabricated A0 mapping. Record that exclusion in the revised plan. |
| F-02 | accepted | Require every `x-onecall-a0-models` entry to be an exact schema-qualified name from the A0 44-table catalog. Expand shorthand/wildcards, including all required `catalog_order.*` entries, and make static validation reject short names, wildcards and wrong schemas. |
| F-03 | accepted | Add a distinct `ShareCodeRotation` response. It may return the new extraction code once but can never return or reconstruct `capabilityToken`; add generated-type validation for that exclusion. |
| F-04 | accepted | Model ordinary frozen IVR outcomes as `200 IvrDecision`, including in-progress/reject/confirmation-not-allowed/consent-required/direct-authorization non-dial results. Reserve `409` for true identity/fingerprint conflicts that prevent a frozen decision. |
| F-05 | accepted | Require current `service_activation.status = ACTIVE` for C10 and define a stable denial. Clarify C6 also requires current active activation and no other `ACTIVE`/`UNKNOWN` call before it creates a task. |
| F-06 | accepted | Add the minimal state-conditional create/replace operations for DRAFT catalog-version and trial-policy-version candidates, with no generic CRUD or independent offer withdrawal. Recompute endpoint counts and S2 scope. |
| F-07 | accepted | Give C9 deterministic sort keys and bind its opaque cursor to the normalized filter digest. |
| F-08 | accepted | Allow P3 an already-admitted, valid session while the grant is `LOCKED`; `LOCKED` blocks only new P2 admission. Preserve generation, expiry, risk and member-witness checks. |
| F-09 | accepted | The plan mandates `/api/v1/**` with `servers: /`, but all 49 inventory rows use unversioned paths. Update every operation path and route example to its exact `/api/v1/...` form, retain `servers: /`, and add a row-by-row/static audit that proves all five surface prefixes and no unversioned operation path remain. |
| F-10 | accepted | C6/C8 cannot reuse IVR decision reasons as HTTP Problem codes while the global registry forbids them. Define distinct customer-surface Problem codes for active-call conflict, outbound-task conflict and non-cancellable task state; remove `TASK_CONFLICT`/`CALL_IN_PROGRESS`/`TASK_CLAIMED`/`TASK_UNAVAILABLE` from C6/C8 Problem mappings and preserve those values only as IVR decision reasons. |
| F-11 | accepted | O18-O21 must not map to `platform.idempotency_record`: A0 fixes its scope registry to `IVR_CONFIRM` and `PURCHASE`. Remove that A0 mapping and the unsupported `Idempotency-Key` contract. Use state-conditional idempotency from the existing natural unique keys plus canonical stored-content comparison for creates, and `expectedRevision` plus requested/current content comparison for replacements; same semantic replay returns current/first DRAFT and changed content returns the existing stable DRAFT conflict/stale code. Do not add an A0 scope, table or transaction. |
| F-12 | accepted | Make O1 deterministic: `createdAt desc, subjectId desc`, bind the cursor to the normalized documented filter digest, and enumerate `CURSOR_INVALID`/`CURSOR_FILTER_MISMATCH`. Keep filters structured and exclude full-number search. |
| F-13 | accepted | Make path/count validation slice-aware. Shared Redocly rules must always enforce versioned approved prefixes and route uniqueness, while an explicit stage-aware check asserts the exact S1, S2 and final S3 operation sets/counts. Each slice must invoke its own reproducible check command; final 49 counts must not make S1/S2 fail. |

### A1 re-review clarification adjudication

- `servers` is required, not optional: the canonical document must contain exactly the approved relative root `url: /`; validation must reject missing, absolute or additional server roots.
- Inventory summaries must not use wildcard/bare error placeholders. Expand C4 `CLAIM_*`, C10 `PURCHASE_CONSENT_*`, and bare O4/O11/O14 409s into the exact approved external Problem codes used by the registry/docs/15.
- `RecordingDeclaration.upload.credential`, `ShareAdmission.mediaAuthorization.credential` and `MediaAuthorization.credential` are response credentials and must be `readOnly` plus `x-onecall-log-redaction: required`; no return-once claim is added unless the underlying replay semantics require it.
- For `ORGANIZATION_MEMBER`, P1 without a matching authenticated customer session may expose only verification/challenge metadata, not shared content metadata; P2/P3 retain the admitted recipient witness and tenant checks.
- The O20 provisional non-null `published_by_employee_id` is an A0 persistence mismatch, because A0 defines it as the actual publisher fact. A1 must not expose it, relabel it as draft creator, or claim the API solved the schema issue. Record it as a runtime prerequisite/residual owned by A0 schema/catalog-order before runtime trial-policy draft creation; the A1 work unit may still specify the public request/response contract and static mapping but must make no runtime claim.

### A1 bounded plan-fix requirements

- Resolve the review open questions within the plan where they are contract-shape decisions: P1 is observation-free; a P2 ticket failure after committed admission is fail-closed `503` and replay reissues without recounting; P3 is the credential-issuance/reconstruction control plane while the media gateway validates the credential and `ORGANIZATION_MEMBER` reissue remains bound to the admitted recipient witness.
- Correct OpenAPI secrecy annotations: response-only capability token/generated code use `readOnly` plus `x-onecall-return-once`; incoming verification secrets use `writeOnly`.
- Keep `limit` default `20` and max `100` only as explicitly labelled V1 API-governance defaults, owned by Backend/API governance rather than security or product policy.
- Recompute all operation and slice counts from the revised inventory. The intended bounded shape is customer 18, operations 21, IVR 6, public-share 3 and callback 1 (49 total), but the plan author must verify the exact inventory rather than treat this as a substitute for the calculation.
- Allowed plan-fix edits: `docs/gateflow/a1-production-openapi-surfaces-spec-plan.md` and one new `docs/gateflow/a1-production-openapi-surfaces-spec-plan-fix-*.md` artifact. No commits, push, PR, A0/source-doc/prototype/screenshot/review-artifact modification or later-gate action.
- F-09 is a bounded follow-up to plan-fix-1: all 49 inventory paths and route examples must use their exact `/api/v1/**` forms while `servers: /` remains unchanged. Static validation must audit the full set. It must not change operation counts, endpoint semantics, A0 mappings, slices or non-goals.

- Design source: `docs/12-technology-stack-and-system-architecture.md`, with current business rules referenced there in `docs/09-business-activation-and-lean-admin.md`, `docs/10-cross-channel-rules.md`, `docs/11-account-identity-and-workspaces.md`, and `docs/13-ivr-call-task-and-inbound-design.md`.
- Motivation: the repository contains reviewed prototypes and design artifacts but no production Maven project, database migrations or production service code. The architecture explicitly requires the production data model and Flyway initial schema specification before implementation.

## Proposed goal contract

- Goal: produce a code-generation-ready PostgreSQL 16 data model and initial Flyway schema specification covering the first production baseline for account/login identity, organization membership, employee identity, business subject/number binding, consent/activation, IVR task/session/call linkage, trial/entitlement/reservation/usage, catalog/order/payment, evidence/media metadata, sharing authorization, idempotency, Outbox and append-only audit facts.
- Success signal: every table and invariant has a single owning module; identifiers, sensitive-field handling, state/revision columns, foreign keys, uniqueness/concurrency constraints, immutable snapshots, deletion policy and migration order are explicit; the specification maps critical cross-channel and IVR state rules to database-enforceable or transaction-enforced controls; unresolved production inputs have an owner and destination instead of guessed values.
- Scope boundary: specification and validation only. The work unit may add the schema design document and its Gateflow artifacts/control updates.
- Non-goals: no executable Flyway migration, Maven/Spring project, API implementation, UI changes, cloud resources, vendor credentials, runtime deployment, production data import or speculative microservice split.
- No over-design: use the approved modular-monolith/PostgreSQL boundary and only model facts required by the current business baseline. Provider-specific configuration, capacity numbers, retention periods and future online workflows remain deferred.

## Project-level constraints

- PostgreSQL is the persistent business truth; Redis cannot own permanent authorization or entitlement facts.
- Module owners cannot write another module's tables directly.
- Existing HTML prototypes and `shared/onecall-business.js` are behavior/QA evidence, not a production schema or SDK.
- Natural-person accounts, login identities, organization memberships, business subjects/numbers and operations employees remain distinct.
- IVR task claim, rights reservation and frozen dial authorization are one transaction; a normalized caller has at most one non-terminal dial task.
- Trials, evidence, activation transitions, payment events, entitlement grants and idempotency records require stable uniqueness and immutable historical facts.
- Recording bytes remain in private object storage; PostgreSQL stores immutable object identity, version and hash metadata.
- Work orders, refunds and invoices remain offline support processes for this release.

## Slice principle

Implementation slices must be behaviorally verifiable and should minimize Gateflow review cost. The planning Agent must not split mechanically by file or table owner; if more than three slices are proposed, it must explain why they cannot be merged safely.

## Worktree boundary

The pre-existing modified QA screenshots and untracked `docs/reviews/plan-review-20261021-205118.md` are outside this project-execution work unit. They must remain unstaged and must not be changed, deleted or committed by Phaseflow Agents.

### Plan review finding adjudication

| Finding | Decision | Controller rationale and required destination |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PR-001 | accepted | The stated S1 -> S2 -> S3 order has real forward dependencies. Fix must produce an acyclic, behaviorally verifiable dependency graph; prefer three slices unless a fourth is proven necessary. |
| PR-002 | accepted | Activation plus first-trial issuance is required to be atomic, while trial/grant/balance belong to `entitlement-metering`. Fix must define an owner-preserving published service participating in the same transaction and its failure/replay tests. |
| PR-003 | accepted | Direct DTMF authorization lacks the canonical-caller lock and a proof that it is the no-task `ENTER_CALLEE` path. Fix must close both concurrency and trust-boundary gaps. |
| PR-004 | accepted | One order-level `entitlement_status` cannot own per-line/per-installment issuance state without a cross-module write. The controller authorizes a minimal `entitlement_metering.entitlement_issuance` fact/current-state table and a baseline increase from 43 to 44 tables if the fix confirms it is the smallest complete model. |
| PR-005 | accepted | D18/INV-35 claim event-domain uniqueness that the listed IVR and usage keys do not implement. Fix must make domain identity and allowed same-string cross-domain behavior explicit. |
| PR-006 | accepted | Activation replay lacks a request fingerprint/first-result binding and operations transitions lack a mandatory action-event identity. Fix must define both. |
| PR-007 | accepted | The `call_id` chain does not name FK parents or intentional deferred/no-FK cases. Fix must specify parent, creation timing and migration wave for each link. |
| PR-008 | accepted | Per-campaign uniqueness permits a second trial campaign despite the permanent `FIRST-TRIAL` rule. Fix must constrain the baseline first-trial identity and test alternate keys/version changes. |
| PR-009 | accepted | Evidence creation ownership is only one-way. Fix must specify the reciprocal creation-event constraint and atomic insertion behavior. |
| PR-010 | accepted | A committed admission followed by Redis failure has no durable retry-key/ticket reconstruction contract. Fix must preserve count-once semantics without making Redis business truth. |
| PR-011 | accepted | A missing authoritative call-end event can strand or incorrectly release a reservation. Fix must define reconciliation states/fields, owner, late-event behavior and tests. |
| PR-012 | accepted | Count checks do not prove catalog-to-wave-to-slice completeness, uniqueness, lifecycle coverage or FK order. Fix must add deterministic structural validation with named missing/extra output. |
| PR-013 | accepted | `catalog_product` cannot be both append-only/frozen and a mutable draft-deletion exception. Fix must choose one lifecycle contract and align deletion/revision validation. |
| PR-014 | accepted | The chosen DB-enforced ownership model depends on cross-schema functions. Fix must either specify a safe, atomic privilege/function contract or simplify the enforcement claim; implementation must not guess `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path`, grants or replay/redaction behavior. |

The 43-table and 35-invariant counts were plan-internal review constraints, not Goal Confirmation terms. The fix may change those counts only as required by accepted findings and must update all counts, catalogs, waves, slices and validation assertions consistently. No other scope expansion is authorized.

A new eventId with the same request fingerprint returns the existing decision and frozen action and completes that event's idempotency row with the existing decision ID; changed input returns a stable conflict without consent/trial/task/reservation side effects. The SUSPENDED rejection applies only before confirmation; never transition an already `CONFIRMED` session to `REJECTED` or insert a second decision. |
| F-11 / new | accepted | Independent review `docs/reviews/plan-review-20261022-140542.md` confirms the missing stable active-call branch. Both entries must test another `ACTIVE/UNKNOWN` call with the same canonical caller, excluding the current `callId`, and freeze `REJECT_CALL_IN_PROGRESS` without task claim/reservation. Define a schema-compatible rejected call/decision write set; do not rely on a raw unique violation. |
| F-12 / new | accepted | The same review confirms the missing typed frozen response. Name the action-specific response fields, nullability, reason codes, committed reservation sources, authorization cutoff/replay behavior and callee encryption/redaction boundary. Preserve the existing IVR action enum and the 44/40/9/3 baseline. |

### User-approved IVR contract refinement

- User instruction: after the shared-action-contract explanation, the user approved optimizing it on 2026-10-22. This authorizes the targeted plan correction and independent re-review, within the existing specification-only A0 scope.
- One IVR-owned action-selection/application-service contract must serve initial resolve and post-activation decisions; external endpoints and immutable decision purposes stay separate. No generic workflow engine or new module/table/slice is needed.
- Authenticate and validate immutable caller/session identity before disclosing a replay. Look up the existing call/purpose decision before suspension checks and before consent/trial/claim/reservation effects. Explicitly define canonical semantic fingerprints (exclude transport event ID), event-key conflicts and rollback of any new idempotency row on semantic conflict.
- Exclude the current call from the other-active-call guard. Reuse the same current permissions, effective entitlement and atomic claim predicates for both entry modes. Risk blocking for number claims/historical evidence is not automatically a blanket ban on future calls; any call-blocking predicate must come from the approved access/lifecycle policy.
- Preserve first resolve replay, the 30-second configurable task TTL, no task-path callee confirmation, IVR-only automatic outbound execution after committed authorization, and frozen B-leg startup cutoff semantics.
- Current request is the targeted contract optimization; the controller will record its re-review outcome and the next A0 gate without claiming production behavior has been implemented.

Required regression tests: valid pending task with zero balance; workspace membership or creator authorization revoked during the prompt; subject risk/access changes to a blocking state during the prompt; task replacement/cancellation during the prompt; expiry between recheck and conditional claim; different eventId with identical fingerprint on active activation; different eventId with changed consent/prompt; repeat after operations suspension with a confirmed session; same eventId replay. Keep existing structural script, F-08 suspended tests, 44/40/9/3 validation and all previously accepted PR/F findings intact.

Fix round 4 is plan-only. Allowed Agent writes are the plan and new fix-4 artifact; preserve earlier fix/review artifacts. The controller owns this control document and must not be edited by the Agent. No SQL/migration, production code, prototype, cloud or credential changes; no stage, commit, push, PR, merge or later gate.

- Task label: `a0-plan-fix4-claude-ds-20261022-01`; runtime/provider: Claude Code / `ds` / DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash. The DeepSeek key was forwarded only in the child process environment and was not printed or persisted.
- Initial wrapper invocation exited `2` before model execution: `claude-agent-run: positional prompt cannot be combined with --prompt or --prompt-file`.
- One same-provider retry exited `2` before model execution due to WSL shell argument expansion (`bash: line 1: =Work: command not found`; `claude-agent-run: --prompt requires a value`). This retry used an environment-transferred Base64 prompt. The run directory contains no result or stderr files, and the fix-4 plan/artifact were not created by the Agent.
- Per the `sub-agents` retry contract, no further same-provider retry is allowed in this dispatch. The user explicitly designated DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, so the controller did not silently switch providers. The task is waiting for a corrected runner invocation path or explicit provider direction; the fix gate remains incomplete.

## Residual risks

- Tencent Cloud region/AZ, resource sizing, COS access mode, WORM/KMS product selection, retention, RPO/RTO and cost remain assigned to later security/capacity/production-readiness work units.
- SIP/line details, IVR media format and carrier-room topology remain assigned to the IVR protocol and integration work units.
- Legal retention, deletion rights and production recording prompts remain assigned to legal/security review and must not be invented in the schema specification.
- The Codex runner's DeepSeek Responses write-tool result correlation is incompatible in the current setup; owner is Agent tooling, destination is runner adapter repair. A verified Claude/DeepSeek runner is the current bounded-gate workaround.

Both UUID components use canonical lowercase UUID text; a revoked row and its replacement use the same key. The owning writer may discover its immutable pair from its own row before locking, then re-read after the advisory lock; a create already has the pair. Under the pair lock, readers query the unique non-revoked relation and require ACTIVE; absent/SUSPENDED/REVOKED fails closed. No new column, cross-module DML/row-lock grant, discovery loop, SQL function or privilege widening is needed. Update all namespace references and assert absent/create/revoke/re-grant serial orders. This is smaller and covers more states than granting an ID plus a changing-row discovery protocol.
- F-11 `accepted`: explicitly validate existence of target subject, authenticated account and supplied organization before appending any claim outcome. Missing referenced IDs yield stable no-row outcomes; inactive existing parents may yield rejected rows only when all foreign keys and the binding/proof reference are valid. No new table or weakening of FKs; preserve risk-before-success and identity conflict confidentiality.
- F-12 `accepted`: a create encountering its own DISABLED binding returns stable `BINDING_DISABLED_CONFLICT` with no mutation; explicit re-enable remains a separate action. An ACTIVE identical binding alone may be an idempotent create result; RETIRED remains terminal. Align unique-loser mapping and both create/re-enable serial orders; never return a disabled row as active success.
- OQ-1 `accepted` as identity clarification: duplicate comparison includes verification method as well as the full canonical reference identity; method changes cannot reuse/disclose the old accepted result.
- OQ-2 `accepted` as transaction clarification: choose a whole-transaction rollback and bounded retry from the start for a request-ID unique loser, reacquiring locks and rechecking current risk/lifecycle/identity. Never query an aborted transaction or retain a tentative owner write. State the exhaustion outcome; do not add a savepoint to S1's no-subtransaction contract.
- OQ-3 `accepted` as scope clarification: T-S1-04 workspace listing is an advisory read-only projection, not an authorization lease. It uses no lifecycle advisory locks; every protected operation revalidates current authority and side-effecting transactions use the shared lock contract. Limit the broad section 9.0/10.8 reader statement to authorization/mutation transactions. Session revocation design remains Q1/Q19; do not invent an authentication subsystem.
- OQ-4 `deferred-with-owner`: S2 author / S2 IVR transaction and test matrix. Place canonical-number acquisition after canonical-caller but before subject/session/call/activation locks whenever needed; preserve the accepted surrounding order. S1 fix records the explicit handoff without authoring S2.
- OQ-5 `accepted` as enforcement of the existing fail-closed scope: S1 subject creation starts with both owner columns null (including OPERATIONS source); optional workspace/source cannot choose ownership. The authorized verified personal claim can set the owner in the same transaction or later. Remove S1 runtime INSERT privileges on owner columns and unsupported organization-owner UPDATE privilege; no S1 path may establish a fresh organization owner. Keep columns/FKs and immutable legacy enterprise relation reuse; enterprise create/transfer remains Product/Subject-number's deferred policy. This does not invent enterprise ownership rules.
- OQ-6 `accepted` as timestamp clarification: claim `verified_at` is the accepted claim decision timestamp at/after `risk_checked_at`, not the original proof-attempt time; proof timing belongs to the immutable verification reference. Align dictionary, transaction and tests without adding columns.
- OQ-7 `accepted` as enforcement of the existing integrity assertion: before setting a null owner, inspect accepted claims for that subject under the subject lock; any existing accepted relation inconsistent with the null/current owner yields `CLAIM_DATA_INTEGRITY`, no repair/new accepted claim/owner change. Assert the seeded-corruption case; do not add a repair workflow.
- OQ-8 `accepted` as navigation-only: correct the current specification citation to docs/12 section 7.2. Historical fix-1/review artifacts stay immutable; record correction in the new artifact.
- OQ-9 `deferred-with-owner`: Product/Subject-number and Organization-access / enterprise ownership and role-permission policy. S1 does not narrow the accepted role set by guessing policy; no fresh enterprise claim or ownership is authorized, and legacy reuse still requires all explicit owner/current-access predicates. No new behavior is claimed.

Security/Q12 owns OTP lifecycle/parameters; absent approved trusted verification input fails closed. No OTP service/schema or invented identity proof in A0.
4. OQ-4 `accepted` clarification: permit LOCKED -> REVOKED/EXPIRED through existing owner revoke/expiry; preserve terminality. LOCKED -> ACTIVE remains extraction-code rotation only; no invented non-code unlock action/policy.
5. OQ-5 `accepted` clarification: plaintext random capability is returned once. Lost creation response retry returns metadata only; a usable replacement requires a new creationRequestId/new grant, with optional existing revoke of the unusable grant. No recoverable token, reissuance action or weakening of digest-only storage.
6. OQ-6 `accepted` clarification: specify canonical action identities and fingerprints for revoke/rotate/expire/extend/lock in existing SHARE_ACCESS events. Same action retry resolves the first fact before mutable revision/state preconditions; changed input conflicts. Stable request/source revision/expiry identities cannot depend on the post-update current revision. No new table/function/API action.
